(23)

Note that since
r
is monoidal, the image of
\sigma
in
\mathcal{T}_{\times}
is automatically pullback. We now note that if
f
is
r
-cartesian, then
f^{\prime}
is
r
-cartesian by
[
10
, Prop. 2.4.1.7]
, and if
f
is
r
-cocartesian, then
f^{\prime}
is
r
-cocartesian (hence
r|\langle 1\rangle
-cocartesian) by the interchange law.
∎

Proposition 5.2.4
.

For any monoidal Beck-Chevalley fibration, there is a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Span}^{\prime}(\mathcal{X})^{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Span}(\mathcal{T})^{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

where
\pi
and
\varpi
are symmetric monoidal categories and
\rho
exhibits
\mathrm{Span}^{\prime}(\mathcal{X})^{\otimes}
as a
\mathrm{Span}(\mathcal{T})^{\otimes}
-monoidal category. Straightening, one finds a lax monoidal functor

\hat{r}\colon(\mathrm{Span}(\mathcal{T}),\widetilde{\times})\to(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty},\times)

with the following description up to equivalence.

•

On objects, the functor
\hat{r}
sends
t\in\mathrm{Span}(\mathcal{T})
to the fiber
\mathcal{X}_{t}\in\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}

•

On morphisms,
\hat{r}
sends a span
t\overset{g}{\leftarrow}s\overset{f}{\to}t^{\prime}
to the composition
f_{!}\circ g^{*}\colon\mathcal{X}_{t}\to\mathcal{X}_{t^{\prime}}
.

•

The structure morphisms

\mathcal{X}_{t}\times\mathcal{X}_{t^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{X}_{t\times t^{\prime}}

of the lax monoidal structure on
\hat{r}
are given by the restriction of the tensor product
\otimes
to the fibers over
t
and
t^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Recall the triple structure
(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}_{\dagger},\mathcal{T}^{\dagger})
from the proof of
Proposition
5.1.10
. We further define a triple structure
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}_{\dagger},\mathcal{X}^{\dagger})
as follows.

•

\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}_{\otimes}
.

•

\mathcal{X}_{\dagger}=(\mathcal{X}_{\otimes})_{\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\simeq}
.

•

\mathcal{X}^{\dagger}
consists only of
r
-cartesian morphisms.

One sees that this is adequate, since pullbacks of the necessary form exist by the usual procedure:

•

Map the diagram down to
\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}^{\mathrm{op}}
, take the pullback there.

•

Take a relative pullback in
\mathcal{T}_{\times}
.

•

Take an
r
-cartesian lift to produce an
r
-relative pullback in
\mathcal{X}_{\times}
. This lies over a pullback in
\mathcal{T}_{\times}
, hence is a pullback.

Thus, we have a map of adequate triples
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}_{\dagger},\mathcal{X}^{\dagger})\to(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}_{\dagger},\mathcal{T}^{\dagger})
.
The fact that this map satisfies the conditions of
Theorem
1.6.2
follows immediately from
Lemma
5.2.3
. This gives us a cocartesian fibration

\rho\colon\mathrm{Span}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}_{\dagger},\mathcal{X}^{\dagger})\to\mathrm{Span}(\mathcal{T},\mathcal{T}_{\dagger},\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}).

Combining this with the map
\pi
of
Proposition
5.1.10
and pulling back along the map
\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathrm{Span}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}_{\dagger},\mathcal{F}^{\dagger})
gives us the triangle

is ambigressive pullback if and only if each component is ambigressive pullback. The pullback part of the condition holds because of the equivalence

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

In this section, we construct this monoidal structure explicitly.

For our construction, will need to upgrade the twisted arrow category construction to a functor

\mathbf{Tw}\colon\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,2)}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty},

where
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,2)}
is the
\infty
-category of
\infty
-bicategories. To this end, we note that we can certainly express the twisted arrow category as an ordinary functor

\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{sc}}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{+}.

Here we take
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{sc}}
and
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{+}
to carry their standard model structures.

Lemma 5.3.1
.

The functor
\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}
preserves weak equivalences between fibrant objects.

Proof.

Let
f\colon\mathbbm{C}\to\mathbbm{D}
be a weak equivalence between fibrant objects in the scaled model structure. Thus,
f
is a bicategorical equivalence between
\infty
-bicategories. Denote the map on the underlying quasicategories by
\mathring{f}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
. We note that
\mathring{f}
is an equivalence of quasicategories.

We consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}(\mathbbm{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}(\mathbbm{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathring{f}\times\mathring{f}^{\mathrm{op}}}

where the square formed is pullback, and
h
is the map guaranteed us by the universal property of the pullback. We note that since
p
is a cartesian fibration,
g
is a weak equivalence since
\mathring{f}\times\mathring{f}^{\mathrm{op}}
is. Thus, in order to show that
\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}(f)
is a weak equivalence, it suffices to show that
h
is.

Since
h
is a morphism
q\to r
of cartesian fibrations, in order to show that it is an equivalence it suffices to check that it is a fiberwise equivalence. Fix some object
C=(c,c^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
. The restriction of
h
to the fibers of
\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}(\mathbbm{C})
and
\mathcal{P}
over
C
is the map

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathbbm{C}}(c,c^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Map}_{\mathbbm{D}}(f(c),f(c^{\prime})).

This is a weak equivalence for all
C
because
f
is an equivalence of
\infty
-bicategories.
∎

By
Theorem
1.5.4
, there is a right Quillen equivalence
G\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{\mathbf{MS}}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{sc}}
. Composing this with the functor
\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}
above gives us a functor

\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}\circ G\colon\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{\mathbf{MS}}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{+}.

Since
G
is a right Quillen functor, it preserves weak equivalences between fibrant objects. Thus, the composite
\mathbf{Tw}^{\prime}\circ G
does as well. Restricting to fibrant objects and taking simplicial localizations yields a functor

\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{\mathbf{MS}}[W^{-1}]\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{+}[W^{-1}].

By
[
8
, Example 1.3.4.8]
, we have equivalences of
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{\mathbf{MS}}[W^{-1}]\simeq\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,2)}
and
\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}^{+}[W^{-1}]\simeq\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
, giving us our functor

\mathbf{Tw}\colon\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,2)}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}.

We are now ready to construct a model for the monoidal twisted arrow category.

.

\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
admits a Cartesian monoidal structure, which can be expressed as a commutative monoid
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{\times}\colon\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{(\infty,2)}
. The starting point of our construction is the composition

Note that this composition yields a commutative monoid in
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
since the functor
\mathbf{Tw}
preserves products. The relative nerve of this composition is a CSMC
\mathbf{Tw}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})_{\otimes}\to\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}^{\mathrm{op}}
with the following description: an
n
-simplex
\sigma
corresponding to a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tw}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})_{\otimes}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}

corresponds to the data of, for each subset
I\subseteq[n]
having minimal element
i
, a map

\tau(I)\colon\Delta^{I}\to\mathbf{Tw}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})^{i}

such that For nonempty subsets
I^{\prime}\subseteq I\subseteq[n]
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{I^{\prime}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tw}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})^{i}}

commutes.

Example 5.3.3
.

An object of
\mathbf{Tw}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})_{\otimes}
lying over
\langle n\rangle
corresponds to a collection of functors
\mathcal{C}_{i}\to\mathcal{D}_{i}
,
i\in\langle n\rangle^{\circ}
.

consists
7

A morphism of the above form is cartesian if and only if the corresponding simplex
\Delta^{3}_{\dagger}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
is thin.

Lemma 5.3.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small 1-category (and, by abuse of notation, its nerve), let
F
,
G\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}\mathrm{et}_{\Delta}
be functors, and let
\alpha\colon F\Rightarrow G
. Suppose that
\alpha
satisfes the following conditions.

1.

For each object
c\in\mathcal{C}
,
\alpha_{c}\colon F(c)\to G(c)
is a cartesian fibration.

2.

For each morphism
f\colon c\to d
in
\mathcal{C}
, the map
Ff\colon F(c)\to F(d)
takes
\alpha_{c}
-cartesian morphisms to
\alpha_{d}
-cartesian morphisms.

Then taking the relative nerve gives a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{F}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{G}(\mathcal{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}

with the following properties.

1.

The maps
\Phi
,
\Gamma
, and
\rho
are all cartesian fibrations.

2.

The
\rho
-cartesian morphisms in
N_{F}(\mathcal{C})
admit the following description: a morphism in
N(F)(\mathcal{C})
lying over a morphim
f\colon d\leftarrow c
in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
consists of a triple
(x,y,\phi)
, where
x\in F(d)
,
y\in F(c)
, and
\phi\colon x\to Ff(y)
. Such a morphism is
\rho
-cartesian if the morphism
\phi
is
\alpha_{d}
-cartesian.

Proof.

We first prove 2. We already know
[
10
, Lemma 3.2.5.11]
that
\rho
is an inner fibration, so it remains only to show that we can solve lifting problems

is

. But by assumption

-cartesian, so this lifting problem has a solution.

Now we show 1. The assumption that each
\alpha_{c}
is a cartesian fibration guarantees that we have enough cartesian lifts, so
\rho
is indeed a cartesian fibration. The maps
\Phi
and
\Gamma
are cartesian fibrations by definition.
∎

Since the category
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
admits products, it admits a cartesian monoidal structure, which we can write as a commutative monoid
G_{1}\colon\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
. We can equally view the cartesian monoidal structure as a cocartesian monoidal structure on
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{op}}
, giving us a commutative monoid
G_{0}\colon\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
. Taking these together gives a commutative monoid

G=G_{0}\times G_{1}\colon\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty};\qquad\langle n\rangle\mapsto(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{op}})^{n}\times\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{n}.

For each
\langle n\rangle\in\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}^{\mathrm{op}}
there is a cartesian fibration
\alpha_{n}\colon\mathbf{Tw}(\mathbbm{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})^{n}\to(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{n})^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{n}
, which form the components of a natural transformation
\alpha
from the functor

F\colon\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty};\qquad\langle n\rangle\mapsto\mathbf{Tw}(\mathbbm{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})^{n}

to the functor
G
.

We now apply the relative nerve to the data
\alpha\colon F\Rightarrow G
. Because the pointwise product of any number of thin 1-simplices in
\mathbf{Tw}(\mathbbm{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})
is again a thin 1-simplex in
\mathbf{Tw}(\mathbbm{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})
, the conditions of
Lemma
5.3.5
are satisfied. Unrolling, we find a commuting triangle of cartesian fibrations

Because both
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}^{\times}\to\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}
and
\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{\times}\to\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}
classify the same functor
\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
, they are related by a fiberwise equivalence. Composing a commutative monoid
\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{\times}
with this equivalence allows us to express symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories as commutative monoids in
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}^{\times}
.

5.4
The monoidal category of local systems

In this subsection, we show that the monoidal structure on the twisted arrow category, defined in
Subsection
5.3
, can be used to construct a monoidal structure on the category of local systems.

Construction 5.4.1
.

We will denote the full subcategory of
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty,\times}
on those objects
[\mathcal{D}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{D}_{n}]
such that
\mathcal{D}_{i}
is an
\infty
-groupoid for all
1\leq i\leq n
by
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}
. Note that
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}\to\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}
can be understood as the relative nerve (as a cartesian fibration) of a commutative monoid
\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
giving the cartesian monoidal structure on
\mathcal{S}
.

Fix some monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
which admits colimits, and such that the tensor product
\otimes\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
preserves colimits in each slot. We express this monoidal
\infty
-category as a commutative monoid
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\colon\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}^{\times}
. Using this, we define a functor

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty,\times}\times(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}^{\times})^{\mathrm{op}}

which is the inclusion
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty,\times}
on the first component of the product, and given by the composition

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}\to\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes})^{\mathrm{op}}}{\to}(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}^{\times})^{\mathrm{op}}

on the second. Forming the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Tw}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})_{\otimes}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty,\times}\times(\widetilde{\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}}_{\infty}^{\times})^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}

gives us a commutative triangle

\hbox to174.96pt{\vbox to50.97pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 87.47911pt\lower-25.48613pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-87.47911pt}{-20.3264pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

We claim that
\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{\otimes}
is a CSMC,
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}
is a cartesian CSMC. Since the (cartesian) relative nerve construction produces a cartesian fibration by definition, the map
p
is a cartesian fibration; by its definition, it is even a CSMC. Furthermore, because the map
r
is a pullback of the horizontal map in
Diagram
24
, it is a cartesian fibration. Hence
q
is also a cartesian fibration. It remains only to show that
q
is a CSMC. It suffices to show that
r
is a cartesian fibration of
\infty
-operads. Note that because both
p^{\prime}
and
q^{\prime}
are CSMCs,
r^{\prime}
is a cartesian fibration of
\infty
-operads. The claim follows because
r
is a pullback of
r^{\prime}
.

Example 5.4.2
.

An object of
\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{\otimes}
lying over
\langle n\rangle
corresponds to a collection of functors
X\to\mathcal{C}
,
i\in\langle n\rangle^{\circ}
.

Example 5.4.3
.

A morphism in
\mathbf{Tw}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})_{\otimes}
lying over the active map
\langle 1\rangle\leftarrow\langle 2\rangle
in
\mathcal{F}\mathrm{in}_{*}^{\mathrm{op}}
consists of

•

A ‘source’ object
F\colon X\to\mathcal{C}

•

A pair of ‘target’ objects
G_{i}\colon Y_{i}\to\mathcal{C}
,
i=1
,
2
.

•

A morphism
\mathbf{Tw}(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty})
corresponding to a diagram
\Delta^{3}_{\dagger}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
with front and back

A morphism of the above form is
r
-cartesian if and only if the corresponding simplex
\Delta^{3}_{\dagger}\to\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
is thin, and
q
-cartesian if and only if the corresponding simplex is thin, and
\alpha
is an equivalence.

Example 5.4.4
.

Examining the
q
-cartesian case more closely, one sees that the tensor product of two local systems
F\colon X\to\mathcal{C}
and
G\colon Y\to\mathcal{C}
is the local system given the composition

F\otimes G\colon X\times Y\to\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}\overset{\otimes}{\to}\mathcal{C}

Lemma 5.4.5
.

The tensor product of local systems preserves cocartesian edges (in the sense of
Definition
5.1.4
).

Proof.

In order to show that the tensor product of local systems preserves cocartesian edges, it suffices by
Lemma
5.1.5
to check that the tensor product of two
r|\langle 1\rangle
-cocartesian edges is again
r|\langle 1\rangle
-cocartesian. Let
e\colon F\to G
and
e^{\prime}\colon F^{\prime}\to G^{\prime}
be
r|\langle 1\rangle
-cocartesian edges of
\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{\otimes}
.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

We wish to show that
e\otimes e^{\prime}
is
r|\langle 1\rangle
-cocartesian.

The tensor product
e\otimes e^{\prime}
is given, up to homotopy, by the pasting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}

In order to show that this map is
r
-cocartesian, we need to show that the outer triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times Y^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\times F^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\times f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes\circ(G\times G^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times G^{\prime}}

exhibits
\otimes\circ(G\times G^{\prime})
as the left Kan extension of
\otimes\circ(F\times F^{\prime})
along
f\times f^{\prime}
. But this follows from the fact that
\otimes
preserves colimits in both slots.
∎

Proposition 5.4.6
.

The map
r
above is a monoidal Beck-Chevalley fibration.

Proof.

We need to check the conditions (M1)–(M5) of
Definition
5.2.1
. The condition (M1) is the content of
Proposition
4.0.3
. Conditions (M2), (M3), and (M5) follow immediately from
Lemma
5.1.6
, using the fact that
r
is a cartesian fibration. That (M4) holds is the content of
Lemma
5.4.5
.
∎

Corollary 5.4.7
.

There is a lax monoidal functor

\hat{r}\colon(\mathrm{Span}(\mathcal{S}),\widetilde{\times})\to(\mathcal{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty},\times),

with the following description up to equivalence.

•

On objects, the functor
\hat{r}
sends a space
X
to the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{X}
of
\mathcal{C}
-local systems on
X
.

•

On morphisms, the functor
\hat{r}
sends a span of spaces
X\overset{g}{\leftarrow}Y\overset{f}{\to}X^{\prime}
to the pull-push

f_{!}\circ g^{*}\colon\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{X}\to\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{\prime}}.

•

The structure morphisms of the lax monoidal structure are the maps

\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{X}\times\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{X^{\prime}}\to\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{X\times X^{\prime}}

given by the composition

\mathrm{Fun}(X,\mathcal{C})\times\mathrm{Fun}(Y,\mathcal{C})\overset{\times}{\to}\mathrm{Fun}(X\times Y,\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{C})\overset{\otimes}{\to}\mathrm{Fun}(X\times Y,\mathcal{C}),

under the identification
\mathbf{LS}(\mathcal{C})_{X}\cong\mathrm{Fun}(X,\mathcal{C})
.
